=== idx: 421 ===
Gold supporting: it is generally accepted that dna methyltransferases carry out specific and non - overlapping functions, dnmt3a and dnmt3b being responsible for the establishment of methylation around the time of implantation and dnmt1 ensuring that methylation is faithfully copied to daughter cells via what has come to be known as " maintenance methylation. === idx: 709 ===
Gold supporting: the proficient repair of the nontranscribed strand cannot be explained by the dedicated subpathway of transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ), which is targeted to the transcribed strand in expressed genes.
LLM supporting:  the transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ) targets both dna strands, but with a particular emphasis on the non - template strand. this is because the non - template strand is the one that is being transcribed, and thus, any damage on this strand can directly interfere with the transcription process. tcr is a mechanism by which rna polymerase ii ( rnapii ) pauses and halts the transcription when it encounters a dna lesion, leading to the recruitment of the nucleotide excision repair ( ner ) machinery. the ner complex then removes the damaged segment of dna, allowing rnapii to resume transcription once the lesion has been repaired. this process ensures that the genetic information is accurately transcribed into mrna, thereby maintaining the integrity of gene expression. tcr is distinct from other forms of ner, such as global genome ner, which can occur independently of active transcription. however, tcr is often considered a subset of the broader ner pathway, as it shares many of the same core components and mechanisms, but is specifically coupled to the transcription process.
Coverage: 0

=== idx: 957 ===

LLM supporting:  the context provided does not contain a specific sentence that directly addresses the gene strand targeted by transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ). however, based on the general understanding of tcr and its mechanism, tcr primarily targets the non - template ( coding ) strand of dna. this is because tcr is initiated when rna polymerase encounters a lesion in the template strand, which blocks transcription. the stalling of rna polymerase at the lesion allows for the recruitment of the tcr machinery, which includes components of the nucleotide excision repair ( ner ) pathway. the ner pathway then removes the lesion from the non - template strand, allowing for the resumption of transcription. while tcr is more efficient in removing certain types of dna damage, such as pyrimidine dimers, its involvement in other forms of dna damage, including those caused by oxidative stress, is still under investigation.
